To be more quantitative, we say that a constant depends on the model if it can be specified as a function of the following:An upper bound for the left-hand side of (1.8).A lower bound for the left-hand side of (2.6).An upper bound for the left-hand side of (1.15).A lower bound for the smallest positive eigenvalue of the covariance matrices of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (F(0), \mathcal {D}_j F(0)), \quad j=1,2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(F(0),DjF(0)),j=1,2.Importantly, estimates for GWHF formulated in terms of model-dependent constants remain valid if F is replaced with the twisted shift \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {T}_{\xi }F(z)= F(z-\xi )e^{i \textrm{Im}(z \bar{\xi })}$$\end{document}TξF(z)=F(z-ξ)eiIm(zξ¯). The corresponding zero sets are related as follows.

An upper bound for the left-hand side of (1.8).

An upper bound for the left-hand side of (1.8).

A lower bound for the left-hand side of (2.6).

A lower bound for the left-hand side of (2.6).

An upper bound for the left-hand side of (1.15).

An upper bound for the left-hand side of (1.15).

A lower bound for the smallest positive eigenvalue of the covariance matrices of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (F(0), \mathcal {D}_j F(0)), \quad j=1,2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(F(0),DjF(0)),j=1,2.

A lower bound for the smallest positive eigenvalue of the covariance matrices of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (F(0), \mathcal {D}_j F(0)), \quad j=1,2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(F(0),DjF(0)),j=1,2.

Remark 2.5

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F: \mathbb {C} \rightarrow \mathbb {C}$$\end{document}F:C→C vanishes at \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z \in \mathbb {C}$$\end{document}z∈C then a direct computation shows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {T}_{\xi } F$$\end{document}TξF vanishes at z+ξ z+ξ and\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \kappa _{z+\xi } (\mathcal {T}_{\xi } F) = \kappa _z (F). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}κz+ξ(TξF)=κz(F).

Poincare index

We consider a GWHF F and the total charge \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\kappa _{\Omega }$$\end{document}κΩ (2.1) of F on a domain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset \mathbb {C}$$\end{document}Ω⊂C. As a first step towards the proof of the hyperuniformity of the charge statistics, we derive a variant of Poincare’s index formula adapted to the twisted derivatives.

Lemma 3.1

Let F be a GWHF satisfying the general assumptions, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset \mathbb {C}$$\end{document}Ω⊂C be a compact domain with smooth boundary, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u \in \mathbb {C}$$\end{document}u∈C. Then the following hold almost surely. (i)The level-crossings of F are non-degenerate, i.e., 3.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {{\,\textrm{Jac}\,}}F(z) \not =0, \qquad z \in \{F=u\}\cap \Omega . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}JacF(z)≠0,z∈{F=u}∩Ω.(ii)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{F=0\} \cap \Omega $$\end{document}{F=0}∩Ω is finite and does not intersect \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial \Omega $$\end{document}∂Ω.(iii)(Covariant Poincare’s index formula) 3.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \kappa _{\Omega } = \frac{1}{\pi } |\Omega | + \frac{1}{2\pi i} \int _{\partial \Omega } \left( \frac{\mathcal {D}_1 F }{F} \,dz + \frac{\mathcal {D}_2 F }{F} \,d\bar{z}\right) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}κΩ=1π|Ω|+12πi∫∂ΩD1FFdz+D2FFdz¯.

(i)The level-crossings of F are non-degenerate, i.e., 3.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {{\,\textrm{Jac}\,}}F(z) \not =0, \qquad z \in \{F=u\}\cap \Omega . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}JacF(z)≠0,z∈{F=u}∩Ω.

The level-crossings of F are non-degenerate, i.e., 3.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {{\,\textrm{Jac}\,}}F(z) \not =0, \qquad z \in \{F=u\}\cap \Omega . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}JacF(z)≠0,z∈{F=u}∩Ω.

(ii)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{F=0\} \cap \Omega $$\end{document}{F=0}∩Ω is finite and does not intersect \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial \Omega $$\end{document}∂Ω.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{F=0\} \cap \Omega $$\end{document}{F=0}∩Ω is finite and does not intersect \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial \Omega $$\end{document}∂Ω.

(iii)(Covariant Poincare’s index formula) 3.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \kappa _{\Omega } = \frac{1}{\pi } |\Omega | + \frac{1}{2\pi i} \int _{\partial \Omega } \left( \frac{\mathcal {D}_1 F }{F} \,dz + \frac{\mathcal {D}_2 F }{F} \,d\bar{z}\right) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}κΩ=1π|Ω|+12πi∫∂ΩD1FFdz+D2FFdz¯.

(Covariant Poincare’s index formula) 3.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \kappa _{\Omega } = \frac{1}{\pi } |\Omega | + \frac{1}{2\pi i} \int _{\partial \Omega } \left( \frac{\mathcal {D}_1 F }{F} \,dz + \frac{\mathcal {D}_2 F }{F} \,d\bar{z}\right) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}κΩ=1π|Ω|+12πi∫∂ΩD1FFdz+D2FFdz¯.

Proof

For part (i), we invoke [5, Proposition 6.5]. The required hypotheses are that F be \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^2$$\end{document}C2 almost surely, as we assume in (1.14), and that the probability density of F(z) be bounded near u uniformly on z, which in our case holds because F(z) is a circularly symmetric complex Gaussian vector with variance \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{Var}\,}}[F(z)] = H(0)=1$$\end{document}Var[F(z)]=H(0)=1 and bounded mean by (1.8).

For part (ii), we use Kac-Rice’s formula to conclude that there is a measurable function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _1:\mathbb {C}\rightarrow [0,\infty )$$\end{document}ρ1:C→[0,∞) such that for any Borel set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E \subset \mathbb {C}$$\end{document}E⊂C:3.3\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E}\big [\#\{F=0\} \cap E\big ] = \int _E \rho _1\, dA. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[#{F=0}∩E]=∫Eρ1dA.Concretely, we invoke [5, Theorem 6.2], a version of Kac-Rice’s formula for Gaussian random fields that requires: (a) F to be almost surely \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^1$$\end{document}C1, which is granted by (1.14); (b) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{Var}\,}}[F(z)]$$\end{document}Var[F(z)] to be non-zero for all z, which is granted by (1.11); (c) the non-degeneracy condition (3.1). The function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _1$$\end{document}ρ1 can be expressed as a certain conditional expectation [5, Theorem 6.2], but we shall not need this fact. Simply, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial \Omega $$\end{document}∂Ω has null Lebesgue measure, we use (3.3) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E=\partial \Omega $$\end{document}E=∂Ω to learn that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {E}\big [\#\{F=0\} \cap \partial \Omega \big ]=0$$\end{document}E[#{F=0}∩∂Ω]=0, and thus \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\#\{F=0\} \cap \partial \Omega =0$$\end{document}#{F=0}∩∂Ω=0 almost surely.

For part (iii), we note that (3.1) means that 0 is a regular value of F. We invoke Poincare’s index formula [45, 58] \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\kappa _\Omega = \frac{1}{2\pi i} \int _{\partial \Omega } \frac{dF}{F}$$\end{document}κΩ=12πi∫∂ΩdFF and deduce (3.2) as follows:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 2\pi i \cdot \kappa _\Omega&= \int _{\partial \Omega } \frac{dF}{F} = \int _{\partial \Omega } \left( \frac{\partial F }{F} \,dz + \frac{\bar{\partial } F }{F} \,d\bar{z}\right) \\&= \int _{\partial \Omega } \left( \frac{\mathcal {D}_1 F }{F} \,dz + \frac{\mathcal {D}_2 F }{F} \,d\bar{z}\right) + \frac{1}{2} \int _{\partial \Omega } \bar{z} \,dz - \frac{1}{2} \int _{\partial \Omega } z \,d\bar{z} \\&=\int _{\partial \Omega } \left( \frac{\mathcal {D}_1 F }{F} \,dz + \frac{\mathcal {D}_2 F }{F} \,d\bar{z}\right) + i\cdot \Im \left[ \int _{\partial \Omega } \bar{z} \,dz\right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}2πi·κΩ=∫∂ΩdFF=∫∂Ω∂FFdz+∂¯FFdz¯=∫∂ΩD1FFdz+D2FFdz¯+12∫∂Ωz¯dz-12∫∂Ωzdz¯=∫∂ΩD1FFdz+D2FFdz¯+i·ℑ∫∂Ωz¯dz.Finally, by Green’s theorem,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Im \left[ \int _{\partial \Omega } \bar{z} \,dz\right] = \int _{\partial \Omega } \left( x\,dy - y\,dx\right) = \int _\Omega 2 \,dxdy = 2 |{\Omega }|, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ℑ∫∂Ωz¯dz=∫∂Ωxdy-ydx=∫Ω2dxdy=2|Ω|,which gives (3.2). □ □

Motivated by Lemma 3.1, we look into correlations between the quotients \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}_j F/F$$\end{document}DjF/F, and derive several technical estimates.

Let F be a GWHF satisfying the general assumptions. Then the following hold. Proof

Part (i). The mean of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}_j F(z)$$\end{document}DjF(z) is bounded by (1.8) and its variance is bounded by Lemma 2.1 and (1.15), which gives (4.1), while (4.2) follows in turn by Hölder.

Part (ii). The Gaussian random vector (F(z), F(w)) has mean \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(F_1(z), F_1(w))$$\end{document}(F1(z),F1(w)) and covariance4.5\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Gamma (z,w) = \begin{bmatrix} 1 & H(z-w) e^{i \textrm{Im}(z \bar{w})} \\ \overline{H(z-w)} \cdot e^{-i \textrm{Im}(z \bar{w})} & 1 \end{bmatrix}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Γ(z,w)=1H(z-w)eiIm(zw¯)H(z-w)¯·e-iIm(zw¯)1.To prove (4.3) we invoke the Hardy-Littlewood rearrangement inequality:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E}\big [ \big | F(z) \big |^{-p} \big ]&= \frac{1}{\pi } \int _{\mathbb {C}} |u+F_1(z)|^{-p} e^{-|u|^2} \,dA(u)\\&\le \frac{1}{\pi } \int _{\mathbb {C}} |u|^{-p} e^{-|u|^2} \,dA(u) =: C_p <\infty , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[|F(z)|-p]=1π∫C|u+F1(z)|-pe-|u|2dA(u)≤1π∫C|u|-pe-|u|2dA(u)=:Cp<∞,because p<2p<2. Suppose that |H(z-w)| < 1/2|H(z-w)|<1/2. Suppose now that |H(z-w)| \ge 1/2|H(z-w)|≥1/2 and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha := H(z-w) e^{i \textrm{Im}(z \bar{w})}$$\end{document}α:=H(z-w)eiIm(zw¯). Then,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[|F(z)·F(w)|-p]=1π2∫C2|u+F1(z)|-p|αu+(1-|α|2)1/2v+F1(w)|-pe-|u|2e-|v|2dA(u)dA(v)=1π2∫C2|u+F1(z)-α-1F1(w)|-p|αu+(1-|α|2)1/2v|-pe-|u-α-1F1(w)|2e-|v|2dA(u)dA(v)≤1π2∫C2|u+F1(z)-α-1F1(w)|-p|αu+(1-|α|2)1/2v|-pe-|v|2dA(u)dA(v)=1π2∫C|u+F1(z)-α-1F1(w)|-p∫C|αu+(1-|α|2)1/2v|-pe-|v|2dA(v)dA(u).Using \begin{document}$$C_p$$\end{document}Cp is a constant depending only on p that might change from line to line. Corollary 4.2

Let F be a GWHF satisfying the general assumptions. Then there exist model-dependent constants C,L>0C,L>0 such that4.7\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E}\Big [ \big | \tfrac{\mathcal {D}_j F(z) }{F(z)}\big |\Big ] \le C, \qquad z \in \mathbb {C}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[|DjF(z)F(z)|]≤C,z∈C,and4.8\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E}\Big [ \big | \tfrac{\mathcal {D}_j F(z) }{F(z)} \cdot {\tfrac{\mathcal {D}_k F(w) }{{F(w)}}} \big | \Big ] \le C, \qquad z,w \in \mathbb {C}, |z-w| > L. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[|DjF(z)F(z)·DkF(w)F(w)|]≤C,z,w∈C,|z-w|>L.

Proof

Let us choose 1<p<21<p<2, say p=3/2p=3/2, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q \in (2,\infty )$$\end{document}q∈(2,∞) be its Hölder conjugate, 1/p+1/q=11/p+1/q=1. By Hölder and Lemma 4.1,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E}\Big [ \big | \tfrac{\mathcal {D}_j F(z) }{F(z)}\big |\Big ] \le \mathbb {E}\Big [ \big | \mathcal {D}_j F(z) \big |^q\Big ]^{1/q} \cdot \mathbb {E}\Big [ \big | F(z)\big |^{-p}\Big ]^{1/p} \le C. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[|DjF(z)F(z)|]≤E[|DjF(z)|q]1/q·E[|F(z)|-p]1/p≤C.Similarly, by (1.9) and (1.15), there exists a constant L>0L>0 such that |H(z-w)|<1/2|H(z-w)|<1/2 for |z-w|>L|z-w|>L and Lemma 4.1 implies\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E}\Big [ \big | \tfrac{\mathcal {D}_j F(z) }{F(z)} \cdot {\tfrac{\mathcal {D}_k F(w) }{{F(w)}}} \big | \Big ] \le \mathbb {E}\Big [ \big | {\mathcal {D}_j F(z) } \cdot {\mathcal {D}_k F(w) }\big |^q \Big ]^{1/q} \cdot \mathbb {E}\Big [ \big | {F(z) } \cdot {F(w) }\big |^{-p} \Big ]^{1/p} \le C'. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[|DjF(z)F(z)·DkF(w)F(w)|]≤E[|DjF(z)·DkF(w)|q]1/q·E[|F(z)·F(w)|-p]1/p≤C′.□ □

Proposition 4.3

Let F be a GWHF satisfying the general assumptions. Then there exist model-dependent constants L,C>0L,C>0 such that the following bound holds for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j,k\in \{1,2\}$$\end{document}j,k∈{1,2}:4.9\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Big | {{\,\textrm{Cov}\,}}\Big [ \tfrac{\mathcal {D}_j F(z) }{F(z)} , {\tfrac{\mathcal {D}_k F(w) }{{F(w)}}} \Big ]\Big | \le C (1+|z-w|)^{-2}, \qquad |z-w| >L. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|Cov[DjF(z)F(z),DkF(w)F(w)]|≤C(1+|z-w|)-2,|z-w|>L.

Proof

Throughout the proof, we fix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j,k\in \{1,2\}$$\end{document}j,k∈{1,2}.

Step 1. Assume first that the random vectors \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(F(z), \mathcal {D}_j F(z))$$\end{document}(F(z),DjF(z)), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(F(w), \mathcal {D}_k F(w))$$\end{document}(F(w),DkF(w)) have non-singular covariance matrices, and denote them by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_j, A_k \in \mathbb {C}^{2\times 2}$$\end{document}Aj,Ak∈C2×2. Step 3. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_j, A_k$$\end{document}Aj,Ak and Γ (z,w)Γ(z,w) be the covariance matrices of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V^*_j$$\end{document}Vj∗, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V^*_k$$\end{document}Vk∗ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V^*$$\end{document}V∗ respectively, and consider again the decomposition (4.11) and (4.12). Variance of aggregated charge

We now state and prove the following more precise version of Theorem 1.1.

Theorem 5.1

Let F be the GWHF (1.1) and assume (1.8), (1.9), (1.11), (1.12), (1.14), and (1.15). Then there exists a model-dependent constant c such that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sup _{w \in \mathbb {C}} \sup _{R \ge 1} \tfrac{1}{R} \textrm{Var} \Big [ \sum _{|z-w| \le R} \kappa _z \Big ] \le c < \infty . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}supw∈CsupR≥11RVar[∑|z-w|≤Rκz]≤c<∞.

Proof

We restrict attention to the ball \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B=B_R(0)$$\end{document}B=BR(0). The conclusions then extend to balls centered at any point because the distribution of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_0$$\end{document}F0 is invariant under twisted shifts (1.3), while all assumptions on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_1$$\end{document}F1 also hold for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {T}_\xi F_1$$\end{document}TξF1 and the constants we rely on are model dependent, cf. Remark 2.5.

Using (3.2) we formally expand the variance as5.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&4 \pi ^2 \cdot {{\,\textrm{Var}\,}}\big [ \kappa _{B} \big ]\\&\quad = \int _{\partial B} \int _{\partial B} \Big ({{\,\textrm{Cov}\,}}\left[ \tfrac{\mathcal {D}_1 F(z) }{F(z)} , {\tfrac{\mathcal {D}_1 F(w) }{F(w)}} \right] \,dz \,d\bar{w} + {{\,\textrm{Cov}\,}}\left[ \tfrac{\mathcal {D}_1 F(z) }{F(z)} , {\tfrac{\mathcal {D}_2 F(w) }{{F(w)}}} \right] \,dz \,dw \\&\qquad + {{\,\textrm{Cov}\,}}\left[ \tfrac{\mathcal {D}_2 F(z) }{F(z)} , {\tfrac{\mathcal {D}_1 F(w) }{{F(w)}}} \right] \,d\bar{z} \,d\bar{w} + {{\,\textrm{Cov}\,}}\left[ \tfrac{\mathcal {D}_2 F(z) }{F(z)} , {\tfrac{\mathcal {D}_2 F(w) }{{F(w)}}} \right] \,d\bar{z} \,dw \Big ). \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}4π2·Var[κB]=∫∂B∫∂B(CovD1F(z)F(z),D1F(w)F(w)dzdw¯+CovD1F(z)F(z),D2F(w)F(w)dzdw+CovD2F(z)F(z),D1F(w)F(w)dz¯dw¯+CovD2F(z)F(z),D2F(w)F(w)dz¯dw).We now derive estimates that justify the expansion (5.1) and bound each of the terms in it. We make use of some estimates for convolutions proved in Sect. 10.2.

We recall (4.4) and use (5.5) to further estimate\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E}\Big [ \big |\frac{\mathcal {D}_j F(z) }{F(z)} \cdot {\frac{\mathcal {D}_k F(w) }{{F(w)}}} \big | \Big ]&\lesssim \mathbb {E}\Big [ \big |{F(z)} \cdot F(w) \big |^{-p} \Big ]^{1/p} \\&\lesssim (1-|H(z-w)|^2)^{1/p-1} \le \varepsilon ^{1/p-1} |z-w|^{2(1/p-1)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[|DjF(z)F(z)·DkF(w)F(w)|]≲E[|F(z)·F(w)|-p]1/p≲(1-|H(z-w)|2)1/p-1≤ε1/p-1|z-w|2(1/p-1),where we used the definition (5.2). For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(z,w) \in E_2$$\end{document}(z,w)∈E2, Lemma 4.1 gives \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {E}\Big [ \big |{F(z)} \cdot F(w) \big |^{-p} \Big ]^{1/p} \lesssim 1$$\end{document}E[|F(z)·F(w)|-p]1/p≲1. Thus, we combine (5.5) with Lemma 10.3 to conclude5.8\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\int _{(z,w) \in E_2} \mathbb {E}\big [ \big |\tfrac{\mathcal {D}_j F(z) }{F(z)} \cdot {\tfrac{\mathcal {D}_k F(w) }{{F(w)}}} \big | \big ] \,|dz| \,|dw| \nonumber \\&\qquad \lesssim \int _{z \in \partial B} \int _{\begin{array}{c} w \in \partial B,\\ |z-w| \le L \end{array}} \mathbb {E}\big [ \big |{F(z)} \cdot F(w) \big |^{-p} \big ]^{1/p} \, |dz| |dw| \lesssim R. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∫(z,w)∈E2E[|DjF(z)F(z)·DkF(w)F(w)|]|dz||dw|≲∫z∈∂B∫w∈∂B,|z-w|≤LE[|F(z)·F(w)|-p]1/p|dz||dw|≲R.Together with (5.4), this shows that5.9\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \int _{(z,w) \in E_2} \big |{{\,\textrm{Cov}\,}}\big [ \tfrac{\mathcal {D}_j F(z) }{F(z)} , {\tfrac{\mathcal {D}_k F(w) }{{F(w)}}} \big ] \big | \,|dz| \,|dw| \le C R. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∫(z,w)∈E2|Cov[DjF(z)F(z),DkF(w)F(w)]||dz||dw|≤CR.Step 5. We combine Proposition 4.3 and Lemma 10.2 to estimate5.10\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \int _{(z,w) \in E_3} \big |{{\,\textrm{Cov}\,}}\Big [ \tfrac{\mathcal {D}_j F(z) }{F(z)} , {\tfrac{\mathcal {D}_k F(w) }{{F(w)}}} \Big ] \big | \,|dz| \,|dw|&\lesssim \int _{\partial B} \int _{\partial B} (1+|z-w|)^{-2} \, |dz| |dw| \\&\lesssim \int _{\partial B} \, |dz| \le CR. \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∫(z,w)∈E3|Cov[DjF(z)F(z),DkF(w)F(w)]||dz||dw|≲∫∂B∫∂B(1+|z-w|)-2|dz||dw|≲∫∂B|dz|≤CR.In addition, by Corollary 4.2, (4.8) holds and5.11\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \int _{(z,w) \in E_3} \mathbb {E}\big [ \big |\tfrac{\mathcal {D}_j F(z) }{F(z)} \cdot {\tfrac{\mathcal {D}_k F(w) }{{F(w)}}} \big | \big ] \,|dz| \,|dw| \lesssim R^2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∫(z,w)∈E3E[|DjF(z)F(z)·DkF(w)F(w)|]|dz||dw|≲R2.Step 6. Combining (5.7), (5.9), and (5.10) we conclude that5.12\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \int _{\partial B} \int _{\partial B} \big |{{\,\textrm{Cov}\,}}\Big [ \tfrac{\mathcal {D}_j F(z) }{F(z)} , {\tfrac{\mathcal {D}_k F(w) }{{F(w)}}} \Big ] \big | \,|dz| \,|dw| \lesssim R, \qquad j,k=1,2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∫∂B∫∂B|Cov[DjF(z)F(z),DkF(w)F(w)]||dz||dw|≲R,j,k=1,2.In addition, combining (5.6), (5.8), and (5.11) we conclude that5.13\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \int _{\partial B} \int _{\partial B} \mathbb {E}\big [ \big |\tfrac{\mathcal {D}_j F(z) }{F(z)} \cdot {\tfrac{\mathcal {D}_k F(w) }{{F(w)}}} \big | \big ] \,|dz| \,|dw| \lesssim R^2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∫∂B∫∂BE[|DjF(z)F(z)·DkF(w)F(w)|]|dz||dw|≲R2.Finally, we can justify the expansion (5.1): the interchange of integration and covariance is justified by (5.3) and (5.13). We now bound each of the terms in (5.1) with (5.12) to conclude that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{Var}\,}}\big [ \kappa _{B} \big ] \lesssim R$$\end{document}Var[κB]≲R, as desired. □ □

Regularity of level-crossing statistics

Let F be a zero-mean GWHF satisfying the general assumptions. The goal of this section is to show that level-crossing statistics \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\#\{z \in K: F(z)=u\}$$\end{document}#{z∈K:F(z)=u} depend continuously on the level value u in quadratic mean. For stationary processes, this follows by simply inspecting explicit formulae; see, e.g., [43]. In our case, we will need to develop estimates for the densities of the level-crossing statistics.

Improved covariance bounds in diagonal directions

Under the general assumptions, the vector (F(z), F(w)) is non-degenerate for z\not =wz≠w and we will denote its covariance matrix by6.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Gamma (z,w) = {{\,\textrm{Cov}\,}}[(F(z),F(w))]. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Γ(z,w)=Cov[(F(z),F(w))].By (2.6), the inverse covariance matrix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma ^{-1} (z,w)$$\end{document}Γ-1(z,w) (henceforth, we will write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma ^{-1}$$\end{document}Γ-1 and omit the dependency on z and w if the arguments are clear from context) has norm \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lesssim O(|z-w|^{-2})$$\end{document}≲O(|z-w|-2) (see (6.12) below). We now make the crucial observation that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma ^{-1}$$\end{document}Γ-1 is much better conditioned when acting on diagonal vectors.

Lemma 6.1

Let F be a zero-mean GWHF satisfying the general assumptions. Then for each R>0R>0 there exists a model-dependent constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_R<\infty $$\end{document}CR<∞, such that for distinct \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z,w \in B_R(0) \subset \mathbb {C}$$\end{document}z,w∈BR(0)⊂C:6.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {{\,\textrm{Var}\,}}\big [F(z)-F(w)\big ] \le C_R |z-w|^2, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Var[F(z)-F(w)]≤CR|z-w|2,6.3\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left| \Gamma ^{-1} \cdot (1,1)^\intercal \right| \le \frac{C_R}{\min \{1,|z-w|\}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Γ-1·(1,1)⊺≤CRmin{1,|z-w|}.6.4\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left| (1,1) \cdot \Gamma ^{-1} \cdot (1,1)^\intercal \right| \le C_R. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(1,1)·Γ-1·(1,1)⊺≤CR.(Here, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma =\Gamma (z,w)$$\end{document}Γ=Γ(z,w) is the covariance matrix (6.1).)

Proof

Since z\not = wz≠w, (F(z), F(w)) is a non-degenerate jointly Gaussian vector. Specifically, writing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K(z,w)=H(z-w)e^{i \Im (z\bar{w})}$$\end{document}K(z,w)=H(z-w)eiℑ(zw¯), its covariance is6.5\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Gamma (z,w) = \begin{bmatrix} 1 & K(z,w)\\ \overline{K(z,w)} & 1 \end{bmatrix} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Γ(z,w)=1K(z,w)K(z,w)¯1and has inverse6.6\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Gamma ^{-1}(z,w) = \frac{1}{1-|H(z-w)|^2} \begin{bmatrix} 1 & -K(z,w)\\ -\overline{K(z,w)} & 1 \end{bmatrix}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Γ-1(z,w)=11-|H(z-w)|21-K(z,w)-K(z,w)¯1.A Taylor expansion in z around w gives\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} K(z,w)&= 1+ \partial _1K(w,w)(z-w)+ \bar{\partial }_1K(w,w)\overline{(z-w)}+ O(|z-w|^2) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}K(z,w)=1+∂1K(w,w)(z-w)+∂¯1K(w,w)(z-w)¯+O(|z-w|2)where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _1$$\end{document}∂1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} Boundedness of the intensity functions

We define the one and two point functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho :\mathbb {C} \rightarrow [0,\infty ]$$\end{document}ρ:C→[0,∞], \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau :\mathbb {C}^2 \rightarrow [0,\infty ]$$\end{document}τ:C2→[0,∞] (associated with the level \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u \in \mathbb {C}$$\end{document}u∈C) by6.8\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \rho (z,u)&= \mathbb {E}\left[ |\det DF(z)| \,\big |\, F(z)=u \right] p_{F(z)}(u), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ρ(z,u)=E|detDF(z)||F(z)=upF(z)(u),6.9\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tau (z,w,u)&= {\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \mathbb {E}\left[ |\det DF(z)| \cdot | \det DF(w)|\,\big |\, F(z)=F(w)=u \right] p_{F(z), F(w)}(u,u), & z\not = w \\ 0, & z=w \end{array}\right. }, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}τ(z,w,u)=E|detDF(z)|·|detDF(w)||F(z)=F(w)=upF(z),F(w)(u,u),z≠w0,z=w,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p_{F(z), F(w)}$$\end{document}pF(z),F(w) is the joint probability density of (F(z), F(w)) and the conditional expectation is defined by Gaussian regression; see, e.g., [5, Proposition 1.2]. The two point function is well-defined because (1.12) implies that (F(z), F(w)) is non-singular for z\not =wz≠w.

According to the Kac-Rice formula [1, 5], the one and two point functions provide densities for the first and second factorial moments of the number of level crossings \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{F=u\}$$\end{document}{F=u} within a test set. The following key result estimates the two-point function associated with general level crossings.

Proposition 6.2

Let F be a zero-mean GWHF satisfying the general assumptions. Then for each compact set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K \subset \mathbb {C}$$\end{document}K⊂C there exists a model-dependent constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_K<\infty $$\end{document}CK<∞, such that6.10\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tau (z,w,u) \le C_K, \qquad z,w,u \in K. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}τ(z,w,u)≤CK,z,w,u∈K.Similarly, for each compact set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K \subset \mathbb {C}$$\end{document}K⊂C there exists a model-dependent constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_K<\infty $$\end{document}CK<∞, such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sup _{z,u\in K} \rho (z,u) \le C_K$$\end{document}supz,u∈Kρ(z,u)≤CK.

Proof

We start by considering the bound (6.10) on the second intensity. Let us fix (z,w,u) ∈ K(z,w,u)∈K with z\not =wz≠w. Without loss of generality we assume that K is a ball. Throughout the proof we let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_K$$\end{document}CK denote a finite constant that may depend on K and the model. Other dependencies are noted with further subscripts. The particular values of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_K$$\end{document}CK may change from line to line.

First we consider the factor \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p_{F(z), F(w)}(u,u)$$\end{document}pF(z),F(w)(u,u) in (6.9) and show that6.11\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} p_{F(z), F(w)}(u,u) \le C_K |z-w|^{-2}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}pF(z),F(w)(u,u)≤CK|z-w|-2.Since z\not = wz≠w, (F(z), F(w)) is a non-degenerate jointly Gaussian vector. Specifically, its covariance is given by (6.5) and its determinant satisfies6.12\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \det \Gamma (z,w) = 1-|H(z-w)|^2 \ge c \min \{1,|z-w|^2\}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}detΓ(z,w)=1-|H(z-w)|2≥cmin{1,|z-w|2}.As a consequence, the probability density\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} p_{F(z), F(w)}(u,v)&= \frac{1}{\pi ^2\det \Gamma (z,w)} \exp \Big ({-\big (u,v\big )^* \,\Gamma (z,w)^{-1} \, \big (u,v\big )}\Big ) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}pF(z),F(w)(u,v)=1π2detΓ(z,w)exp(-(u,v)∗Γ(z,w)-1(u,v))satisfies (6.11).

Thus, to prove (6.10), it remains to show that the other factor in (6.9) satisfies\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathbb {E}\left( |\det (DF(z))| \cdot |\det (DF(w)) |\,\big |\, F(z)=F(w)=u \right) \le C_K |z-w|^{2}. $$\end{document}E|det(DF(z))|·|det(DF(w))||F(z)=F(w)=u≤CK|z-w|2.This will be done in several steps.

Step 1. By Cauchy-Schwarz,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\mathbb {E}\left( |\det (DF(z))| \cdot |\det (DF(w)) |\,\big |\, F(z)=F(w)=u \right) \\&\qquad \le \left[ \mathbb {E}\left( |\det (DF(z))|^2\,\big |\, F(z)=F(w)=u \right) \right] ^{1/2} \cdot \\&\qquad \qquad \left[ \mathbb {E}\left( |\det (DF(w))|^2\,\big |\, F(z)=F(w)=u \right) \right] ^{1/2}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E|det(DF(z))|·|det(DF(w))||F(z)=F(w)=u≤E|det(DF(z))|2|F(z)=F(w)=u1/2·E|det(DF(w))|2|F(z)=F(w)=u1/2.Hence, by symmetry in z and w, it remains to show that6.13\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E}\left( |\det (DF(z))|^2\,\big |\, F(z)=F(w)=u \right) \le C_K |z-w|^2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E|det(DF(z))|2|F(z)=F(w)=u≤CK|z-w|2.Step 2. We expand F around z to obtain6.14\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} F(w)&= F(z) + (w-z) \partial F(z) + \overline{(w-z)} \bar{\partial } F(z) \nonumber \\&\quad + (w-z)^2 R_1(z,w) + |w-z|^2 R_2(z,w) + \overline{(w-z)}^2 R_3(z,w), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}F(w)=F(z)+(w-z)∂F(z)+(w-z)¯∂¯F(z)+(w-z)2R1(z,w)+|w-z|2R2(z,w)+(w-z)¯2R3(z,w),where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}R1(z,w)=∫01∂2F(z+t(w-z))(1-t)dt,R2(z,w)=2∫01∂∂¯F(z+t(w-z))(1-t)dt,R3(z,w)=∫01∂¯2F(z+t(w-z))(1-t)dt.We introduce, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v \in \mathbb {C}$$\end{document}v∈C with |v|=1|v|=1, the notation6.15\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} It remains to bound the conditional expectation on the right-hand side of (6.18) by a constant that depends only on K and the model.

Step 3. For a multi-index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha =(\alpha _1, \alpha _2)$$\end{document}α=(α1,α2), we denote \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{(z,\bar{z})}^{\alpha } = \partial ^{\alpha _1} \bar{\partial }^{\alpha _2}$$\end{document}∂(z,z¯)α=∂α1∂¯α2 and let6.19\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E := \sum _{\alpha : |\alpha | \le 2} \sup _{\xi \in K} | \partial _{(z,\bar{z})}^{\alpha } F(\xi )|. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E:=∑α:|α|≤2supξ∈K|∂(z,z¯)αF(ξ)|.Note that the functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R_j$$\end{document}Rj in (6.14), as well as ∂ F(z)∂F(z) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\partial } F(z)$$\end{document}∂¯F(z), satisfy6.20\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |X| \lesssim E, \qquad X \in \{\partial F(z), \bar{\partial } F(z), R_1(z,w), R_2(z,w), R_3(z,w)\}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|X|≲E,X∈{∂F(z),∂¯F(z),R1(z,w),R2(z,w),R3(z,w)}.We will show that for p \ge 1p≥16.21\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb {E}[E^p] \le C_{K,p}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[Ep]≤CK,p.Let us first fix a multi-index α α with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\alpha | \le 2$$\end{document}|α|≤2 and set\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} L^\alpha (\xi ,\chi ) := \mathbb {E}\Big
